- **#306: the eleventh evaluation's report** (branch `docs/eval11-report`): `docs/evaluations/2026-10-23-agent-eleventh-round.md`. All four agents (grades C++, rota Rust, seats Java, tides C) passed their first verify with no wrong value; one false reason (#300). The Mediums were fixed by #307 to #310 and #312, the Lows by #314 and #315. Written at about a third of the tenth report's length.
- **#311: a copy a statement stores through a pointer withholds the local** (branch `fix/vec-copy-through-pointer`; found fixing #300), with tests that fail on `aeb8a6b`, #310's merge: at `+ other();`, the second line of `let t = total(vec![x, 1])`, `x` was `[settable]` and `--set x=100` reported the change, but `t` used the old value. `vec!` stores `x`'s copy in its Box through a raw pointer (`((*_10).1: …) = [move _7, const 1_u32]`), and the copy analysis skipped every store through a pointer. Each local such a store may write into (the pointer's aliases, the Box and the Vec made of it, and what they borrow) now holds what the value holds, so `x` is refused there: "a statement under way at the stop has already read it into a temporary (at line 13)", where the must-analysis follows the store into the Box's own memory. `vec![x]`, a struct or a `String` clone in a `vec!`, and an older rustc's `ShallowInitBox` are refused too; `vec![x; n]`, `Box::new([x, 1])` and `[x, 1]` were already. A store or call that may write into what holds a read ends the must-analysis's claim, so `b[0] = 7;` after `let mut b = Box::new([x, 1]);` no longer gives "the program has already copied `x` into `b`" but a hedge. A copy `*p = x;` stores in `slot` is the program's own: `x` is refused while the program goes on reading it through `p`.
  - **In the same PR:** a refusal before the run ended with `settable here: x` for the very local it refused (`let t = add(x,` / `other());` at its second line), with a test that fails on `aeb8a6b`. `RustSession._settable_names` now leaves out each local `_never_in` rules out at that line in every function it is listed for; where none is left, it says `no root locals`.
  - **Review round 1 of PR #316** (two blocking issues; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `d6659dc`): the must-analysis made a call's result a sure read whenever it was given one, so `total(discard(vec![x, 1]))` / `+ other();`, with `discard` returning `vec![7, 1]`, said "has already read `x` into a temporary" though nothing live held it. A call's result is never a sure copy now, and what a call is given, or a holder it is given a pointer into (`_may_write`), holds none after it; only the standard library's `box_assume_init_into_vec_unsafe`, proven so by `_std_transfers`, passes on what `vec!`'s Box held. So the issue's own line is now a hedge, and a Vec still to be used a fact. The may-analysis missed writes a call makes, and writes through a pointer to memory no local stands for (on `aeb8a6b` too): `v.push(x)` then `v[0]`, `ptr::write(p, x)` into `slot`, `*p = x;` through an argument or `Box::into_raw`'s pointer then `*p` all left `x` `[settable]` with no effect. A call given a `&mut`, a raw pointer or an aggregate holding one may now store what else it is given wherever that pointer reaches, `v` included, as may `copy_nonoverlapping` (not `Box::new` given a closure holding `&s`, which `test_a_borrow_in_a_trait_object_or_a_guards_drop_is_never_missed` caught); where nothing the pointer was made from stands for its pointee, the pointer binding does ("the program's own `p` … may point at a copy of `x`").
  - **Review round 2 of PR #316** (two blocking issues; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `f60d4f4`): a write through a destination took only direct copies, so `v.extend_from_slice(&a)` after `let a = [x];`, and `v.extend_from_slice(&[x])`, left `x` `[settable]` with no effect; a source now counts where it holds a direct copy or points at one (a borrow of such a holder, a cast or aggregate of it, a pointer a call returns from one, as `a.iter()`). `_pointers_to` stopped at `Option<&mut u32>`, so after `let r = v.last_mut().unwrap(); *r = x;` a later `*r` was no use of `v`; it now follows any value that may hold a pointer. Probing more shapes found `m.entry(1).or_insert(x)` and `v.iter_mut().for_each(|r| *r = x)`, whose `Entry` and `IterMut` hold a `&mut` their types don't show (`_may_write` now counts a standard type with a lifetime unless it owns or only reads), and `v.iter_mut().next().unwrap()`, whose refusal didn't name the store's line. `s.push_str(&x.to_string())`, `let y = x.clone();` and `ptr::read(&x)` remain: a value a call computes from a pointer into the local is no copy (#192), since counting it refused `w.sum` in `movavg`, which the next `w.push(v)` reads through `&mut self`. `moved` of `let keep = moved;` is now refused before the run (`keep` goes on with it through `println!`'s `Arguments`), not at the stop.
  - **Review round 3 of PR #316** (one blocking issue; the fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `3e4cd61`): `_may_write` took every standard type with a lifetime to write through a `&mut` it may hold, unless listed as owning or reading, so `observe(a.chunks_exact(1), x)` said "the program's own `a` … may hold a copy of `x`", which no safe program can do; `rchunks`, `split_ascii_whitespace`, `btree_map::Iter` and `path::Components` alike. A callee now writes through an argument only on positive evidence (`Body._may_write_through`): its type shows a writable pointer, or is a mutable standard view (`Entry`, the `IterMut` family, `RefMut`, `Drain`, `ChunksMut`), or something it was made from shows one (a closure or struct capturing `&mut v`).
  - **Review round 4 of PR #316** (one blocking issue; the fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `685ccdf`): provenance lost the access, so `look(&h, x)` with `h = Hold { p: &mut v }` said "`v` may hold a copy of `x`". Through `&Hold`, safe code can't write through that `&mut`, and `main` allowed the change, which worked. Each step of what an argument was made from now notes whether it passed a shared `&` (`Body._reached`). Past one, only a raw pointer or a cell counts (`_may_write`'s `shared`, and `_unshared` within a type: `&&mut Vec<u32>` writes nothing). A struct holding `&mut v` passed by value or as `&mut h`, and `&h` holding a `*mut` or a `&Cell`, are still refused.
  - **Review round 5 of PR #316** (one blocking issue; the fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `c2ade10`): a cell or atomic was found by a match on `Cell<` or `Atomic` anywhere in a type. So `look(&h, x)` with a crate's `NotCell { p: &mut v }` or `Atomicity` said "`v` may hold a copy of `x`"; `main` allowed the change, which worked. Now only a standard path's exact last segment counts (`_shared_writable`): `Cell`, `RefCell`, `UnsafeCell`, `OnceCell`, `OnceLock`, `Mutex`, `RwLock`, `NonNull`, and `Atomic` or `AtomicU32` and the like. `&Cell<u32>` and `&AtomicU32` are still refused.
- **#313: the evidence store works on SQLite 3.30 to 3.40, and refuses an older one** (branch `fix/sqlite-minimum`; found on PR #312), with tests that fail on `5548cd2`: before 3.41, SQLite asks the authorizer about an UPDATE of `sqlite_master` the first time a connection uses `json_each`, `json_tree` or a `pragma_` function, so every canned `diff` failed (`no such table: json_each` on 3.31.1). A reader now uses each once before setting the authorizer, which is unchanged. A store opens only on SQLite 3.30 or newer (an aggregate's `FILTER` needs 3.30) where the JSON functions, `json_each`, `json_tree` and window functions each run, else `UNSUPPORTED` naming what is missing; checked with builds of 3.29 to 3.41 and one without JSON. Before 3.41, `peeky sql` can use only the JSON and pragma table-valued functions; another one's failure says so.
  - **Review round 1 of PR #318** (one blocking issue, one non-blocking; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `d5fc2ec`): a build with scalar JSON but no virtual tables passed the check, then every canned query failed with `no such table: json_each`; the check now runs what Peeky uses.   - **Review round 2 of PR #318** (one blocking issue; the fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `286277b`): that hint went to any `pragma_` name and any module SQLite has, so `pragma_nosuch`, `rtree` and `fts5` on 3.40.1 were told to try 3.41, where they fail too. Now `no such table` gets it only for a known table-valued function this SQLite has (`fts3tokenize`, `dbstat`, ...); a real pragma gets the pragma functions `peeky sql` reads, as newer SQLite refuses it too; anything else gets none.
- **#305: `--set-at LINE@HIT name=@before` writes back the value from before a change** (branch `feat/set-restore`; eleventh evaluation: tides 3), with tests that fail on `61dbc06`: the guard flip that skips a statement takes two `--set-at`s (`15@all trend=0`, `18@all trend=@before`), not six with hand-copied values. A restore is made only where Peeky knows the value exactly: the probe's latest change to that selector, made at the same hit of its own line, in the same frame and call, and read whole at that write (C, C++ and Rust integers; Java integers, booleans and chars; Python numbers, `True`, `False` and `None`). To tell calls apart, the probe also stops at the first line of each function on a restore's line from the start of the run, and refuses a restore where the change's call wasn't seen to begin or a new call began since. Rust async bodies and Python generators, lambdas, comprehensions and class bodies are refused before the run. Each restore is a change in the text (`restored`), JSON (`restores`), summaries and evidence.
  - **Review round 1 of PR #322** (three blocking issues, four non-blocking; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `589710c`): a restore wrote whatever its name named at its stop (Java `b.n` after `b = new Box(9)`, a shadowing `x`, another block's `y`); the worker now keeps which storage a change wrote (a local's declaration, the object holding a Java field) and refuses a restore to any other before writing. `--until hits` could end between an `@all` change and its restore; that restore is now owed, and one whose hit never comes is reported in `unapplied` (`unrestored`), in `set_outcomes` (`complete: false`) and a warning, with exit 4. The new wire fields bump the protocol to 4. A restore over a value other than the one its change wrote says `restored over the current 6` (`overwrote` in JSON). Schema 19 adds `interventions.restores_at`, which `set_effect` shows; `diff`'s and `compare`'s `(sets)` row name each restore. Stops that watch for new calls go to `watch_pauses_ms`, not `set_pauses_ms` or the max pause. AGENT_GUIDE keeps its `program_ended` sentence.
  - **Review round 2 of PR #322** (two blocking issues, one non-blocking; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `dd6b7b5`): what an `@all` restore owed was tied to whether the latest change was a restore, so a later `x=2` after the restore was called left in place (exit 4), and a second restore (undoing the first) was skipped by `--until hits`. Now a restore owes the latest change, a restore's too, only where its line hasn't reached that change's hit, the same for `--until hits` and the end of the run. "restored over the program's 6" credited the program with the probe's own writes through an alias: it says `restored over the current 6`. A restore whose line never stopped is one entry in `unapplied`, with `unrestored`.
- **#320: what a call returns, given a pointer into the local, may be a copy** (branch `fix/call-result-copies`; found fixing #311), with tests that fail on `c027670`, #316's merge: after `let y = x.clone();`, `s.push_str(&x.to_string());` or `let y = unsafe { std::ptr::read(&x) };`, `x` was `[settable]` where the program goes on with the copy, and `--set x=100` was reported though the output kept the old value. What a call given a pointer into the local, or into what holds a copy, returns is now a possible copy where its type may hold a value, and the refusal hedges ("may hold a copy"); a result that only points (`&str`, an iterator) points into it, so `format!`, `v.iter().sum()` and `out.push_str(&name)` count too. Only the standard library's measures and comparisons (`len`, `contains`, `eq`, `cmp`, `partial_cmp`), proven by their standard types and paths, return none, where the local can't hold their `bool`, `usize` or `Ordering`. After `let mut a = x; let b = std::mem::take(&mut a);` the refusal names `b`, not `a`. `movavg`'s `w.sum` is now refused after `let mean = w.push(v);`: on the last pass a change there was never seen.
  - **Review round 1 of PR #323** (two blocking issues; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `99c90f5`): a result type showing any reference was taken to hold no value, so `let h = hybrid(&x);` with `hybrid(x: &u32) -> (u32, &'static ())`, or a struct `Hybrid<'_> { v: u32, r: &u32 }`, left `x` `[settable]`, and `h.0` kept the old copy. Only a type holding nothing but pointers (a reference, raw pointer, `NonNull`, a tuple, array or `Option` of these, a standard pointer view) holds no copy now; the standard library's `Box::new`, `Box::pin`, `Rc::new` and `Arc::new` return only what they are given, which keeps #164's boxed closures true. And `String::len` was taken for the standard library's by its short name, so a crate's own `struct String;` with `fn len(s: &std::string::String)` was exempt; a short name now counts only where the crate gives it no item of its own.
  - **Review round 2 of PR #323** (two blocking issues; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `1e0962a`): a standard view was known by any `std::`, `core::` or `alloc::` path, so a crate's `mod alloc { pub mod str { pub struct Chars<'a> { v: u32, r: &'a () } } }` returned by a call held no copy, and the set was taken while the old value printed; each root now counts only where the crate gives that name nothing of its own (`_std_crates`, `alloc` covered). And `let it = s.chars().rev();` was "the program's own `it` … may hold a copy of `s`"; the standard library's adapter calls (`rev`, `map`, `skip`) return only what they were given, `Rev`, `Zip` and the like of views hold only pointers, a `map` closure holds a copy only where it captured one, and where a result may hold a copy or only point into the local, the refusal says so and offers no copy to change.
  - **Review round 3 of PR #323** (one blocking issue; its fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `471c6c1`): the standard calls were proven all or nothing, so a `mod alloc {}` beside `let n = a.len();` (`core::slice::<impl [u32]>::len`) made `n` "may hold a copy of `x`", a false reason. Each proof now needs only the root it uses: `core::slice` paths `core`, `std::mem::take` and `ptr::eq` their own root, an inherent `Vec::len` the root of its argument's type, and a comparison its trait's root; the advice that calls a `for` item a slice's element needs `std`, the root its iterator is named under.
  - **Merged with PR #325** (#324's branch): both defined a module-level `_callee` in `rust_mir.py`, and #325's, which gives only a call's last path segment, replaced #320's `(path, arguments)`. So no call was proven a standard measure or wrapper, and 7 unit tests of `test_rust_mir_call_copies.py` failed. #320's is `_call_parts` now. #325's `ptr::eq` exemption in `inlined_reads` checks the comparison's own root (`_trusts`, `_comparison_root`).
- **#324: a line breakpoint past a call inlined on its line withholds what the call was given** (branch `fix/inlined-read-before-stop`; found fixing #320), with tests that fail on `c027670`: at a breakpoint on `let y = x.clone();`, `x` was `[settable]` and `--set x=100` reported the change, but the program printed the old value. LLVM inlines `<u32 as Clone>::clone` even at opt-level 0; its load of `x` carries `core/src/clone.rs`'s line and comes before the line's first row of its own, where LLDB (host, 18 and 20) puts the breakpoint, so the stop was past the read. The DWARF's inlined calls now keep their site and callee's name (`CodeBlock.call`). Where the MIR shows no temporary holding the local, a stop with code inlined from its line below its pc (`_inlined_below`) withholds what the MIR call at that column and of that name is given, or what may point into it (`OwnedLocals.inlined_reads`; the standard `ptr::eq` reads no pointee): "line 20's code may already have read `x` before this stop", the row `line 20's inlined `clone` may have read it`. `(*r).clone()`, `p.a.clone()`, your own `#[inline(always)]` functions and a String given to one are withheld alike; `z` of `let y = z.clone() + x;` is, `x` stays settable, and `vec!`'s inlined allocation withholds nothing. A step from the line before stops at the inlined call's first instruction, which `_line_start` now counts as the line's start, so `x` is settable there and the change reaches `y` (on `c027670` it said "part-way through line").
  - **Review round 1 of PR #325** (two blocking issues; each fix with tests that fail on the reviewed head `450976b`): the check took only inlined calls whose DWARF site is the stop's line, so `let y = mclone!(x) + k;` (`macro_rules! mclone { ($v:expr) => {{ $v.clone() }}; }`), whose inlined `clone` names the macro's definition as its site, left `x` `[settable]` and printed `MACRO 6`. The code just before the stop, back to the last row of another statement's line outside inlined code and macro definitions (`_window`, `macro_lines`), now holds no inlined call Peeky can't place (a macro's definition, another file, no site): such a call withholds every local, with no step offered, since LLDB shows that step's stop on the macro's definition. The DWARF's inlined calls of the line must be the IR's (`IrFunction.inlined`), or every local is withheld. And `read_global()`, an `#[inline(always)]` reader of a `static mut G: *const u32` set to `&x`, is given nothing, so `x` stayed `[settable]` (`GLOBAL 6`): in a crate with unsafe code, an inlined call before the stop other than the standard library's `ptr::eq` now withholds every local (`(unsafe code)`), while a step from the line before still allows the change (`GLOBAL 101`).
- **#326: a step that stops in a macro's definition keeps the session** (branch `fix/macro-step-session`; found fixing #324), with tests that fail on `27922f7`: a `session step` from the line before `let y = mclone!(x) + 1;` stops at the inlined `clone`'s first instruction, which LLDB shows on the macro's definition, a line with no row of its own; `session inspect` then failed with DEBUGGER_ERROR "Native stopped line does not match prepared symbols" and closed the session. So did a step onto `bump(&mut x);`, whose only code is an `#[inline(always)]` call. `RustSession.validate_stop` now accepts a line the DWARF names as an inlined call's site in the frame's function, or one in a macro's definition (`macro_definitions`). At a stop in a macro's definition with no row of its own in the function (`macro_stop`), `inspect` reads no value of that frame and says why (`INVALID_REQUEST`, the state's `macro_stop`), and `step` and `continue` go on; nested macros, a macro in another file and `decl_macro` alike. A line of the function's own with no row starts at its lowest inlined call (`_line_start`), never "part-way". A breakpoint on such a line (`--at`, `session start --at`, `continue --to`) is refused before it is placed (`unplaceable_line`), where it was placed on the inlined code (`breakpoint src/main.rs:615 verified`, then DEBUGGER_ERROR, or NO_STOP). Standard-library macros already stepped into std's sources.

**Process notes:**
- Create PRs and issues with `gh api` (REST): `gh pr create`/`gh issue create` GraphQL calls returned 504s, and the app's PR tool needs the project's GitHub link.
- Squash-merge with `gh api -X PUT .../pulls/N/merge -f merge_method=squash -f sha=<head>`.
- **2026-10-17:** the `code-review` agent started failing with "400 The requested model is not supported" (also the general-purpose agent). The last #71 round was verified by hand with real builds and recorded as such in the review doc.
- Never pipe pytest output through `tail` before checking its exit code.
- Changes to workflow files cannot be merged through the API (no `workflow` scope). Validate them by dispatching the workflow, then push to `main` over SSH.
- **2026-10-16:** GitHub Actions stopped starting jobs ("recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased"). The owner was unavailable, so #46 and #47 were merged on equivalent verification:
  - the full local suite, including the remote e2e in OrbStack;
  - every Linux CI lane in Docker (`scripts/ci_linux`, see CONTRIBUTING.md), with the evidence in each PR. Fixing billing is the owner's decision.
- **Docker under OrbStack:** containers that hang on a bind mount cannot be killed. Feed trees with `git archive HEAD | docker run -i …`; if it hangs anyway, `orb stop && orb start`.

**Local Java adapter bundle:** `outputs/peeky/work/java-adapter-step` (String cap and stepping). `~/.peeky/venice/attach-*.json` point to it. Rebuild with `scripts/setup_java_adapter.py --output <absolute path>`. After PR #123 merges, bundles without the `eventhub-log-guard` patch are refused: rebuild them.

## Previous slices


PR #23 merged as `2995958` (Rust worker, active Option views, compiled-test plus checker verification, five review rounds, no blocking issues on the final head `825c075`, all nine lanes green).

Branch `feat/stateless-probe` (pushed; the Copilot PR tool is blocked until the project's GitHub account is re-linked, so open the PR manually). It implements the owner's direction (ADR 0004 and ADR 0005): **Peeky is a standalone tool that models, harnesses and humans call; it never calls a model, and there is no mode switch.** One `peeky` CLI provides `describe` (manifest), `probe` (one stateless question), `session start|wait|inspect|get|set|continue|report|close|list` (step by step across shell commands via a local worker) and `verify` (fresh build plus independent checker). Targets are auto-detected with toolchain discovery. There is a stateless HTTP `probe` plus `Client.probe`, docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md and skills/peeky-debug. 506 unit and integration tests pass locally, along with the real Rust CLI and probe tests.

Java on Venice end-to-end tests (owner request) is **delivered experimentally** on branch `feat/java-attach`, stacked on `feat/stateless-probe`; both are pushed with CI green, and PRs must be opened manually until the account is re-linked. `JavaAttachTarget` attaches over loopback to a JVM started with `gradlew … --debug-jvm`, maps only the declared sources' compiled classes, rechecks hashed sources, classes and adapter on every operation including `wait` and `report`, labels provenance `attached`, refuses edit verification and detaches without killing the JVM. Verified on Venice `ba78edda8`: `peeky probe` at `TestEmptyPush.java:85` captured `storeName`, `sendControlMessageDirectly` and `controllerClient` for both data-provider runs in 28.5 s; Gradle reported both tests PASSED. Root cause of the first failed attempts: thousands of DAP `thread` events evicted lifecycle events. Only lifecycle events are now kept in bounded history.

Venice on Apple Silicon without Rosetta: grpc-java ships only x86_64 macOS code generators. `scripts/venice/native-grpc.sh` builds v1.59.1 natively from a pinned commit with SHA-256 checks and exact patch counts, and writes a Gradle init script. Venice `assemble` plus integration-test classes then succeed (2m23s) with no change to the Venice repo. Recipe in docs/JAVA_ATTACH.md.

Reviews: the CLI and attach review found 3 blocking and 7 should-fix issues, all fixed in `3bd1e38`/`d41bdf7` with regressions (path confinement in verify, `no-stop` probes, provenance on wait/report, hard-capped HTTP probe subprocess, token-authenticated worker shutdown, private validated session records, selector truncation, bounded attach discovery, detection precedence, pinned Venice helper, narrowed docs). A verification pass found five more (HTTP probe child isolation, stop validation, cleanup inside the cap, complete JSON results, full attach entry bounds); these are fixed in `27937b7`, and the final review reported **no blocking issues**. Evidence on `27937b7`: all nine CI lanes passed (run 38691568364, longest 71 s); 562 local tests passed (unit, integration, Java including attach, Rust); and the Venice probe again captured both hits, with both tests PASSED.

Next: open one PR from `feat/java-attach` (it contains `feat/stateless-probe`) and merge after reconfirming lanes on its head. Then: a live-model session where an agent drives `peeky` against a Venice bug using docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md; production-class breakpoints in Venice (add `services/*/build/classes/java/main` roots); ADR 0004 steps 4–5 (query grammar, evidence store); lifecycle #6, corpus #7, SSH #8.

Stacked on `feat/java-attach` (2026-10-15):
- **`feat/error-codes` (#6):** protocol version 1, machine-readable error codes with hints, retryable and session_closed flags ([ERRORS.md](ERRORS.md)), and lifecycle regressions. CI green, run 38694056033.
- **`feat/corpus` (#7):** the ten-bug corpus and distinct outcomes. A live agent evaluation ([evaluations/2026-10-15-agent-cli.md](evaluations/2026-10-15-agent-cli.md)) had three independent agents each reach verified general fixes. Its findings are fixed:
  - Python `--set` is now limited to literals; it previously evaluated expressions inside the debuggee.
  - `verify` cleans up its scratch copy, shows the failure reason and diff, and explains `--old` mismatches.
  - Probes warn about relocated breakpoints and short runs.
  - All failures now use one error object.
  - With every real toolchain lane enabled, 644 tests pass locally.
- **Review:** the independent review of error codes plus corpus found no blocking issues. Its two should-fix issues and one nit are fixed ([reviews/ERRORS_CORPUS_REVIEW.md](reviews/ERRORS_CORPUS_REVIEW.md)).
- **Agent speed ([ADR 0006](adr/0006-agent-speed.md), owner priority):**
  - Probes report `pauses_ms`, and on attached JVMs they end at the last hit.
  - Sessions take `--max-pause` (10 s by default for attached JVMs). The worker auto-resumes overdue stops, even unobserved ones.
  - `probe` and `verify` take `--background`, collected with `peeky job wait` (exit 3 while running).
  - `describe` reports the timing contract.
  - Verified on Venice's timed test: the background probe took 19.2 s, and the slow-agent and absent-agent sessions both auto-resumed. All runs PASSED.
  - Open issue: one Venice session never received a `stopped` event (see JAVA_ATTACH.md). Diagnostics were added.
- **Agent-speed review:** no blocking issues. Three should-fix issues are fixed: background errors are replayed on stderr, a relative state dir now works, and crash stops are never auto-resumed (checked with real LLDB). Four nits are fixed too. See the same review file.
- **Venice production-code breakpoints:** verified. A probe in the client library (`ControllerClient.createNewStore`) and one in the controller's HTTP handler (`CreateStore`, on a Jetty thread) each captured both invocations, and the tests PASSED. The recipe is in JAVA_ATTACH.md.
  - Fixed while doing this: `describe` without `--at` on a multi-source target now describes every source instead of raising a traceback.
  - Every unexpected CLI exception is now a structured error.
  - Missing paths now return `INVALID_REQUEST` with a path hint.
- **Next:**
  - one PR from `feat/corpus` covering the whole stack;
  - SSH separate host (#8; needs a second host);
  - a user-selected non-synthetic program.

Known local-only limitation (pre-existing on main, fixed by #143): test_native_adapter_death_reaps_stopped_target failed with Apple LLDB on macOS (the stopped program outlived lldb-dap) and passed on hosted Linux.

## Owner direction: stateless probes (2026-10-14)

The owner prefers stateless operation where possible and wants heappy-level ease of use. Both modes must be supported: the existing stateful sessions and one-request stateless probes over the same guarded engine, with a `describe` manifest, canned probes and SQL over captured evidence. Recorded as [ADR 0004](adr/0004-stateless-probes.md). This is direction only, not started. Until it lands, new worker features should be expressible as declared probe steps.
